@@135891861 <p> We are gathered here not only to remember a man who , through his thoughts and actions , offered us a new mode of dignity , but also to reflect , again , on what his legacy offers us in the context of Nigeria 's interminable search for good leadership . As the polity stumbles from one error to another tragedy in the so-called nation-building efforts , it is remarkable that we must return to contend with the ideas and the practical demonstrations of leadership of the man with whom Nigeria has been having an unending conversation , Chief Obafemi Awolowo . <p> As kidnappings and killings have become the rituals of daily life in many parts of the polity , with the nation-state itself facing the threat of being kidnapped , it is important to remind ourselves that the theological , economic , social and political provocations and disasters that define this era are mere manifestations of Nigeria 's refusal to reconcile itself to the fundaments of how to build a good society as well as an egalitarian federal democracy which Chief Awolowo , more than any other @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ for a few years demonstrated , with long lasting impact . At the core of the crisis that has engulfed the polity for several decades now is a leadership crisis . This is the reason I would like to reflect today on leadership and what I call " Awolowo 's Theory of Action " in the context of his devotion to politics as future-making . <p> As we experience what would seem to be the normalization of an internal security landscape in which there is a convergence of jihadist insurgency , communal grievances , some under the name of banditry , secessionist agitations , and criminal economies , those who are not familiar with Awolowo 's insight and foresight would need to be reminded that the man with the round frame glasses had warned the ascendant political order of his age that , if there was no course correction , what we are experiencing now would constitute their generational endowment to the political geography that Frederick Lugard imposed in the heart of Africa . <p> As I reflected on this lecture , it struck me that most of what Awolowo @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ concerns the past , though the man was , during his lifetime , mostly concerned with the future . The past was only a useful background for him in redetermining the future . Thus , it is an irony that while this deeply divided country continues to be bogged down by the politics of the past and the conflicting and contrasting narratives of who wronged whom in the past and how we arrived here , what it truly needs is a reflection on the postulations of a man who almost always emerges from our past to constantly remind us about the possibilities of our collective future . For Awo , history-making was in the service of future-making . <p> Some have said that Awolowo came too early for Nigeria . I disagree . He was a man whose most important life mission could be said to have been one of future-making . Leaders who are futuremakers never come too early . This is partly why Chief Awolowo remains relevant today . When we talk about Awolowo 's theory of action and his leadership qualities , which remain unrivaled almost four @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ fact that , ultimately , he was a man concerned fundamentally with Nigeria 's future , Africa 's future , and indeed , the future of the Black people of the world . As he stated in Path to Nigerian Greatness , he made a vow while in Calabar Prison in 1965 : " I hereby dedicate the rest of my life to the service of the peoples of Nigeria , nay , of Africa , by promoting their welfare and happiness .... This is my vow and my pledge , made to God .... ( A ) t this juncture in my life , my one and only ambition is to have an opportunity to live the rest of my life for history , by means of selfless and beneficent service to the peoples of Nigeria , in particular , and of Africa , and the black peoples of the world , in general . " <p> It is clear from this pledge that living his life for history was about making a future for his people , that is , Nigerians , Africans , and the Black people @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ approach to history-making as future-making , I suggest , is particularly critical in an era in which Afro-pessimists seem to have moved from regarding Africans as a " people without history " to a " people without a future . " An overview of the continent 's current conditions leads to distressing conclusions , with Nigeria standing out , given that it possesses not only the largest population but also the most vibrant and highly skilled labor force , even though a substantial percentage of this has migrated abroad . From the resurgence of military adventurers in West and Central Africa , the return of presidents-for-life , if they ever went away , in Central and East Africa , symbolized by the nonagenarian in Cameroon , or the octogenarians in Equatorial Guinea , Congo-Brazzaville , Uganda , and Eritrea , and the younger version in Rwanda , to the relentless sufferings imposed on the people of the continent by interconnected crises arising from governance failures , corruption , poverty , poor infrastructure , unemployment , most worryingly the highest rate of youth unemployment ( roughly one-third of Africa 's 420 @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ looking for jobs ) , and violent conflicts , all worsened by climate change and high dependency on primary resources , the extraordinary capacities of the people of the continent and their insuppressible cheerfulness have been challenged in different ways . <p> Yet , the continent remains salvageable , if not immediately , certainly in the near future . So too is Nigeria , according to Chief Awolowo . Awolowo always believed in the possibility of a glorious future , no matter the odds . This is based primarily on his modernist outlook , which underpinned his progressive politics . Thus , to reflect on the future-making that was central to Awolowo 's public project as a pathway to his theory of action does not constitute a simple politico-ethical response to the tragedy that Nigeria has experienced , or what , even in much better times , was described as the ' squandering of riches . ' Rather , it is to provide us with a programmatic idea and ideal of what is to be done when a polity and its people confront such a tragedy . Moral outrage , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ clearly throughout his political career . What is important is always to imagine a better future , no matter how bad things are . He was constantly asking , " What are the possibilities of a better future ? " <p> This is why I have decided to focus today on " Politics as Future-Making , " using Chief Obafemi Awolowo 's ideas and his public career as a heuristic lens to examine Nigeria 's unending search for effective leadership . This idea of **25;878;TOOLONG could represent a paradox , given that , classically , politics is the art of the possible . But Awolowo continues to speak from the grave because he decided , while on this side of the divide , never to surrender the future possibilities of politics ( based on long-term thinking , planning , and development ) to the personalized political exigencies of the search for power . <p> The question of temporality was always evident in Awolowo 's reflections . From thinking about what to do with the legacies of the past , both its assets and liabilities , to what kind of society @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ to create a better future , future-making was a focal point of his life 's mission . I think , therefore , that it is important that we use this to reflect on his legacy and re-evaluate the leadership challenges facing Nigeria . Awolowo looked back , only to look forward . He acted in the present of his time , only to ensure a greater future for the people . Thus , for him , the future constitutes , to use the language of some philosophers , an awakening of the present . In this sense , the past , present , and future are less about being ( that is , who or what we are ) , but about becoming , that is our future possibilities . This was why he was a man of incredible hope for the possibilities of a " new dawn . " We recall again his 1963 allocutus , partly a lamentation about what had become , and could become , the lot of what he described as " this great Federation . " Awolowo stated : " For some time to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and the rule of law , will change or might change into utter darkness . But after darkness -- and this is a commonplace -- comes a glorious dawn . " <p> It is important to note here that , contrary to what might seem evident in Nigeria 's political history , Awolowo believed that the " ultimate triumph " of good over evil was " commonplace . " <p> Against the backdrop of his hope for Nigeria 's future , which some frustrated adherents from his region of Nigeria have now rejected and even reinterpreted , perhaps it is useful , in remembering him , to address our minds to a few pertinent questions about Nigeria . Why has Nigeria constantly produced some good leaders in every area of human endeavour , but has not experienced overall sustainable good leadership ? What is it in the total composition and structure of Nigeria that makes it difficult , if not impossible , for sustainable good leadership to emerge ? Why has the country 's evident good human agency ( in areas such as medicine , law , civil advocacy , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ etc. ) been incapable of weakening if now neutralizing the strong and recalcitrant agency of bad national political leadership ? <p> Mr. Chairman , I will be immodest by suggesting that I have some answers . I intend to wrap these answers around the questions raised by Awolowo 's ideas and practice . I therefore invite reflections on these questions in this forum as we commemorate a man who devoted his entire public career not only to addressing these questions but also to demonstrating the limitless possibilities of good leadership . <p> Let me quickly explain what I mean by future-making . It is a proactive , thought-based and practice-oriented process of conceiving , shaping , enacting , and propelling desired future states rather than merely predicting and imagining them . This process of conceiving a collective future and enacting the conditions that would produce such a future involves ( i ) founding , or what others call " active creation , " ( ii ) defining the goals and purposes of that collective future , ( iii ) designing the strategies and tactics necessary to achieving the goals and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ mobilizing the people through various means , including electoral politics , to march towards that future . <p> Awolowo 's vision of the future was captured by the slogan of his first political party , the Action Group : " Freedom for all , life more abundant . " ( This was the impetus for the Yoruba sloganization of that universal ideal as " Afenifere . " ) In the 1950s , this was a revolutionary idea that made Awolowo an enemy to local and foreign forces that would later combine to ensure he never became Nigeria 's leader . In Awolowo 's egalitarian vision , " equality of good fortune " through shareable social projects for all citizens without presupposing equality of outcomes is within the capacity of the modern state . But those who could not contemplate such a world asked : " Freedom for all " and " life more abundant for everyone ? " " Where would that place those of us who are enjoying age-long privileges based on religious or religio-cultural authority ? " " The children of the poor peasants and the children of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ with a level playing field in which they can all thrive ? What a dangerous idea ! " <p> *To be continued <p> *Being text of the public lecture delivered by Prof Wale Adebanwi , the Presidential Penn Compact Professor of Africana Studies , University of Pennsylvania , at Obafemi Awolowo Memorial Lecture at Ikenne , Ogun State , Nigeria , on March 6 , 2026 
@@135891905 <h> Counting the gains of Tinubu 's UK visit , by Mustapha Isah <p> President Bola Tinubu 's recent visit to the United Kingdom is significant in many ways considering the long-standing relationship between the country and Nigeria . Nigeria has maintained a robust relationship with the UK after gaining independence from the colonial masters in 1960 , and for decades developed into strong business and trade partnerships . <p> The visit highlighted Nigeria 's return to the international stage on the strength of the bold economic reforms of the Tinubu administration , which have stabilised Nigeria 's economy , liberalised the investment climate and modernised the tax system , attracting global recognition and commendation . <p> The visit was historic because it came 37 years after a similar visit by former military President , General Ibrahim Babangida , in 1989 . <p> The elaborate reception accorded President Tinubu , his wife , Senator Oluremi Tinubu , and the Nigerian delegation by King Charles III and the Queen Consort , Camilla , at the Windsor Castle , demonstrated the importance of Nigeria to the UK authorities . <p> Nigeria is @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . Trade between Nigeria and the United Kingdom in 2025 reached record levels , with total trade in goods and services hitting ? 8.1 billion in the year ending Q3 2025 , representing an 11.4 percent increase from 2024 . Nigeria 's exports to the United Kingdom are dominated by crude oil , valued at ? 1 billion , while the UK 's exports to Nigeria are led by refined oil , worth ? 1.6 billion . <p> During the visit , a financing deal worth ? 746 million was signed between Nigeria and the United Kingdom for the refurbishment of two major Nigerian ports : Apapa Quays and Tincan Port Complex . The project represents a major step toward modernising Nigeria 's port infrastructure , enhancing trade efficiency , and strengthening the country 's position as a key maritime hub in West Africa <p> The import of that most ambitious deal is unmistakable . The multiplier effect of a modernised port , on the speedy clearance of goods and on general operational efficiency and effectiveness , can not be overemphasised . <p> The Nigerian Government and the United Kingdom @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , border security , and business engagement . They include a Memorandum of Understanding on Migration Partnership , a Statement of Intent on Cooperation on Organised Immigration Crime and Border Security , and a Statement of Intent on the Expansion of Business Visas for UK companies operating in Nigeria . The agreements demonstrate Nigeria 's commitment to a migration framework that is transparent and respectful of national interests and international obligations . <p> This partnership is a testament to Nigeria 's determination to build a migration system that is safe , orderly , and mutually beneficial to both countries . Reducing barriers to legal migration and trade is key to achieving President Tinubu 's $1 trillion economy . The business visa agreement is expected to yield clear benefits for both countries and represents a bold step which is critical for Nigeria 's economic growth . <p> The three agreements are expected to strengthen collaboration on migration management , combat organised immigration crime , and facilitate easier business engagement between the UK and Nigeria . <p> Discussions during the meeting between President Tinubu and British Prime Minister Keir Starmer at 10 @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ climate change , terrorism , the tension in the Middle East , and wider global challenges . Nigeria expects assistance from the United Kingdom in the fight against insecurity based on those talks . <p> Both President Tinubu and King Charles were right when they alluded in their speeches at the state banquet at Windsor Castle to the growing , harmonious , mutually beneficial bilateral and trade relationships between the two countries in their speeches . <p> Yes , President Tinubu 's visit to the United Kingdom was for two days , but the gains will continue to resonate in the coming years . 
@@135892261 <h> How Governor Aliyu is charting Sokoto 's path for the next half-century <p> Still , the future will require scale , coordination , and long-term planning . For instance , agriculture must link seamlessly with energy , infrastructure , finance , and markets . Education must align with the skills demanded by a changing economy -- agribusiness , renewable energy , logistics , healthcare , and technology . Policies must speak to each other and not operate in silos . <p> Education and human capital development form another critical pillar of Governor Aliyu 's vision . Since 2023 , budgetary allocation to education has surpassed the recommended 15% benchmark by UNESCO , underscoring his unwavering commitment to changing the narrative . With a predominantly young population , Sokoto state has serious challenges on its hands : youth can be a powerful dividend or a destabilizing burden . As the saying goes , the idle mind is the devil 's workshop . Governor Aliyu 's emphasis on expanding access to education , strengthening schools , and promoting skills acquisition shows foresight . A child educated today will most certainly become @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . Or , as it is often said , " If you think education is expensive , try ignorance . " The next half-century will undoubtedly favor states that invest strategically and consistently in education . By investing in human capital , Governor Aliyu is preparing Sokoto 's young population to be productive contributors to the economy over the next 50 years . <p> Governor Aliyu deserves applause for ensuring that Sokoto state is economically viable and prosperous in the next 50 years by investing in energy and infrastructure , which are true economic enablers . Without reliable and sustainable power , any industrialisation dream will remain a mirage . <p> Energy , like infrastructure , is also vital . A major milestone in this direction is the completion of the long-abandoned Sokoto Independent Power Project -- a 38-megawatt power plant , delivered at a cost of approximately ? 14 billion . Industrialisation can not thrive without reliable and sustainable power . It is expected that the project will significantly improve the state 's energy capacity , providing dedicated and more stable electricity to critical public institutions , residences , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ play a critical role in the desired industrial growth , improved public services , and private sector confidence in Sokoto state as an investment destination . <p> It is important to highlight the sustained investment in solar energy solutions by the Aliyu administration as part of its commitment to illuminating both urban and rural communities through the installation of solar-powered streetlights . Beyond improving visibility , these initiatives have significantly enhanced nighttime commerce and strengthened security across the state . <p> Equally important is the state 's substantial investment in roads and housing infrastructure . Strategic road expansion has improved trade , reduced the cost of transportation , and connected farmers and producers to markets more easily . Meanwhile , the various large-scale housing developments have stimulated the construction sector , created jobs , and are attracting long-term private investment . <p> Governor Aliyu also understands that the provision of infrastructure must be forward-looking : roads , water systems , housing layouts , and digital connectivity must be designed not merely for today 's population , but for a more urbanised one . Sustainable urban planning , integrated transport networks @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ addressed because they are prerequisites for transforming Sokoto state into a competitive , investment-ready , and economically resilient state over the next half-century . <p> Governor Aliyu has invested heavily and wisely in the security of the state , especially along its border with Zamfara state . Since taking office , he has established the Sokoto Community Corps , built military bases , purchased vehicles and motorcycles , and ensured that security agencies waging war against terrorists receive their allowances . He has also invested in empowerment programs , recognizing that lasting security goes beyond military operations . The governor is clearly determined to end insecurity , aware that the huge resources being spent on security agencies could be channeled to other areas , and mindful of the impact on internally displaced persons ( IDPs ) and his investment drive . By prioritising security , his administration acknowledges a simple truth : no economy can grow where fear dominates daily life , and peace is the soil in which prosperity takes root . <p> One of the greatest tests the administration is addressing is the economic development of the state @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ dependent on federal allocations , it has taken aggressive measures to improve internally generated revenue through efficient tax systems , digital tools , and an expanded formal economy . Collection has improved because the people can see visible projects and the blockage of leakages . Trust , as they say , is the foundation of tax compliance . He has also put together an Economic Advisory Committee that is working on an economic blueprint for the state . <p> The immediate past administration largely neglected the youth population , particularly by abandoning several social intervention programs that were established to equip them with practical skills . For a state with a predominantly young population , sidelining youth-focused development carries significant social and economic consequences . These initiatives , which were introduced during the tenure of former governor Aliyu Wamakko , with the current governor serving as commissioner at the time , were designed to promote self-reliance , entrepreneurship , and create employment among young people . Young people represent not only a demographic majority but also the driving force of productivity , innovation , and long-term stability . It is commendable that programmes that build human capital -- especially among the youth -- under the leadership of Governor Aliyu have been strengthened and expanded over time to ensure lasting progress as the state prepares for the next fifty years . Expanding human capital initiatives will ensure long-term societal and economic benefits . Governor Aliyu has proven that sustainable development is never accidental but the outcome of deliberate policy choices , vision , and strategic investment . <p> No serious conversation about the next fifty years can ignore climate change and the land degradation already shaping daily life -- desertification and the mass movement it has triggered . Going forward , Governor Aliyu must continue his commitment to confronting climate change realities : desertification , water scarcity , and land degradation , which are existential threats . Climate-resilient agriculture , irrigation management , afforestation , and sustainable land-use planning remain the way forward . By addressing environmental sustainability alongside economic growth , the administration is positioning the state to be @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Equally important is the attention being paid to fiscal discipline and good governance processes by Governor Aliyu . Prosperity is not built on ambition alone , but on institutions that plan , budget , implement , and account transparently . It is to the credit of the Aliyu administration that it has refused to borrow from commercial banks to fund the execution of its projects . <p> In planning for the next 50 years , it is important to stress that the state government alone can not carry the weight of the next half-century . Traditional rulers , religious leaders , the private sector , civil society , and Sokoto state citizens in the diaspora all have roles to play . They must team up with the governor , who has extended a hand of fellowship to them , to move the state forward . <p> The Aliyu administration has seized the opportunity that history rarely offers with both hands : to align a symbolic milestone with a strategic reset . The foundations being laid today are important . Perhaps the greatest responsibility facing Governor Aliyu is ensuring that these @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ what endures long after its tenure . Laws , development plans , strong institutions , and policies must be entrenched so they can survive political transitions . The future of Sokoto state can not be subjected to repeated resets . <p> When Sokoto turns 100 in 2076 , today 's leaders will be history . What will remain are the outcomes of today 's choices : the skills of the population , the strength of institutions , the resilience of the economy , and the shared prosperity of its citizens . Governor Aliyu 's current efforts -- the hard decisions , the strategic planning , and the practical interventions -- represent the foundation for this future . <p> Governor Aliyu 's vision , anchored in concrete action , discipline , and long-term planning , offers a model of how strategic leadership can lay the foundation for a resilient , prosperous , and inclusive state .

@@135901200 <h> How rising heatwave could become a top killer in Nigeria <p> Climate change rarely announces itself in ways that are easy to track . It is often framed through dramatic events , floods swallowing communities , droughts crippling farms , storms tearing through coastlines . But for Nigeria , the more defining impact may come in a quieter , less visible form : heat . <p> Not extreme heat as a one-off , but a steady , compounding rise in temperatures that gradually pushes up mortality . <p> This is the central warning from a new report by the Climate Impact Lab , which examines how rising temperatures will shape global death rates by mid-century . According to the report , by 2050 , climate change is projected to increase temperature-related deaths in Nigeria by about 8 per 100,000 people . <p> On its own , that number might not trigger an alarm . Compared to countries like Niger or Burkina Faso , where projected increases exceed 60 deaths per 100,000 , it can even seem modest . <p> Because in Nigeria , the story is not about extremes @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ a health issue The science is straightforward . As temperatures rise , the human body works harder to regulate itself . Prolonged exposure increases the risk of cardiovascular stress , respiratory complications , and overall mortality . Unlike infectious diseases , which can spike and recede , heat operates differently , it raises the baseline . <p> In practical terms , it means more people die than would have otherwise , especially among vulnerable groups : the elderly , those with pre-existing conditions , and millions of Nigerians who work outdoors every day . <p> From traders in open markets to construction workers and farmers , exposure is not occasional , but constant . <p> - Advertisement - <p> This is how climate change begins to blur into public health . <p> Nigeria 's position in the heat belt Geography matters . According to the report , countries closer to the equator are more exposed to rising temperatures , and Nigeria sits firmly within this zone . <p> In cooler regions of the world , warming actually reduces deaths by cutting cold-related mortality . But in already warm countries , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , while any gains from fewer cold days are negligible . <p> - Advertisement - <p> This imbalance is what defines much of West Africa . <p> Across the Sahel , stretching through Mali , Chad , and Mauritania , mortality increases are projected to be among the highest globally . Cities like Niamey and Ouagadougou are already on track to see dramatic spikes in heat-related deaths . <p> Nigeria may not top those rankings , but it shares many of the same structural pressures : rapid urbanisation , population growth , and limited adaptive capacity . <p> In other words , it sits just below the extreme . but well within the danger zone . <p> The inequality factor If heat is the trigger , inequality is the multiplier . <p> The Climate Impact Lab report finds that low-income countries are projected to account for ten times more climate-related deaths than wealthier nations . The reason is not just higher exposure , but lower capacity to respond . <p> In high-income countries , adaptation happens almost automatically . Air conditioning is widespread , healthcare systems are stronger , and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ This creates what researchers describe as an " adaptation gap " , the space between what is needed to stay safe and what people can realistically access . <p> That gap is where mortality rises . <p> Cities as the frontline Nowhere will this pressure be felt more than in urban centres . <p> As Nigerian cities expand , they are becoming hotter . Concrete replaces vegetation , heat is trapped , and night-time temperatures remain high . The so-called " urban heat island " effect is no longer theoretical , it is already shaping daily life . <p> In cities like Lagos , Kano , and Port Harcourt , this translates into longer periods of heat stress , particularly for low-income residents living in dense , poorly ventilated areas . <p> Across West Africa , similar patterns are emerging . In Ghana , for instance , Accra is already seeing measurable increases in temperature-related mortality . <p> The risk is not just that cities will get hotter . It is that they will become environments where heat and inequality reinforce each other . <p> A growing public health burden @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ that climate-related mortality could become one of the leading causes of death in parts of low-income countries . <p> This does not mean it replaces existing health challenges . Rather , it adds to them . <p> Nigeria already contends with malaria , respiratory infections , and non-communicable diseases . Rising temperatures will compound these pressures , increasing the overall burden on individuals and the healthcare system . <p> And unlike many diseases , heat is difficult to diagnose as a cause . Its impact is often indirect , making it harder to track and even harder to prioritise . <p> The narrow window for action There is , however , a pathway to mitigation : economic growth . <p> As incomes rise , so does the ability to adapt , through better housing , improved healthcare , and access to cooling technologies . The report notes that globally , income growth could significantly reduce climate-related mortality by mid-century . <p> For Nigeria , this presents both an opportunity and a risk . <p> If growth is inclusive and translates into improved living conditions , it could act as a @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of the population will remain exposed . <p> Which brings the conversation back to policy . <p> Heat is still a blind spot in Nigeria 's climate response . Flooding , energy , and agriculture dominate the agenda , but temperature-related health risks receive far less attention . <p> Yet the solutions are not abstract . They include : <p> Heat action plans in cities <p> Public awareness systems <p> Urban design that reduces heat exposure <p> Stronger healthcare preparedness <p> None of these are out of reach . But they require recognising heat for what it is , not just a weather condition , but a slow-moving threat to human life . <p> The real risk is not that the impact is invisible . It is that it remains underestimated for too long . <p> Obidike Okafor is an award winning , seasoned journalist and content consultant . Obidike has left his mark on the global stage , writing for prestigious publications in Nigeria , the UK , South Africa , Kenya , Germany , and Senegal . @@135925454 <h> Lassa fever 's five-state trap , the loudest warning <p> After going through my columns on Lassa fever over the last decade , I was forced to accept the glaring truth that there is also a morbid pattern to the outbreak , just like in other climate-induced epidemics like cholera and meningitis . And here lies the pain . If the outbreak is recurring , why are we not able to master it ? Nations around the world are daily devising ways to nip epidemics in the bud . What makes us a human civilisation is that when nature deals us a bad hand , we do not curl up and die -- we get up and win . <p> Lassa fever is preventable because it has a visible source . The rodent that carries the virus is here with us ; all we need to do is think . We just have to devise ways to either do away with the rodents or ensure they never breed in our immediate environment . Better yet , we create means of putting a boundary between the vectors ' ecosystem and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of the year and the weather that aids their interface with the human ecosystem . <p> Sadly , we have allowed it to fester , and it is getting worse by the day . The report for this year 's outbreak shows that the case fatality rate stands at 25.1 per cent , significantly higher than the 18.7 per cent recorded during the same period in 2025 . <p> Since its discovery over five decades ago , Lassa fever has evolved from a mysterious rural illness into one of Nigeria 's most persistent public health threats . Its history is not just a medical timeline -- it is a story of missed opportunities , slow institutional learning , and recurring national vulnerability . It was first identified in 1969 in Lassa town , in present-day Borno State , when a missionary nurse fell ill with a strange hemorrhagic disease . By 1970 , scientists had isolated the virus and described its clinical features . This period marked the birth of awareness , but little was done structurally to prevent future outbreaks . <p> So , it began a silent spread @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ decades , surveillance was weak , public awareness was low , and the disease remained largely rural and underreported . Occasional hospital outbreaks occurred , especially due to poor infection control , but sadly , the disease did not yet command national attention . This error of treating it as a localised anomaly and not a national threat caused a national reemergence as we entered the millennium . By the early 2000s , outbreaks became more frequent and geographically widespread . The disease was evolving faster than the country 's preparedness . Lassa fever transitioned from a sporadic crisis to a seasonal epidemic pattern , typically peaking between October and May . <p> However , rising case numbers revealed a paradox : better detection was exposing a larger underlying problem . By 2023 , Nigeria recorded thousands of suspected cases and hundreds of confirmed infections within just a few months . Meanwhile , it was driven by poverty and a poor environment . Climate and urban pressures are increasing the risk , and sadly , Nigeria is now managing Lassa fever but not reducing its root causes . Progress has @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the purpose of today 's piece is to show a subtle dynamic in outbreak patterns and what we can take out of it . Roughly 85 per cent of reported cases are concentrated in just five states . On the surface , this may appear to be a narrow epidemiological issue . In reality , it is a profound national warning -- one that reveals deep structural weaknesses in governance , healthcare delivery , environmental management , and socio-economic planning . <p> In my mind , if we can interrogate the tapestry of this diabolical pattern with a solution-minded segregation of datasets , there is every chance that we shall develop a more durable national response system than the knee-jerk intervention mechanism we have been deploying before now . The states -- Edo , Ondo , Ebonyi , Bauchi , and Taraba -- are not merely locations on a map . They are a mirror reflecting Nigeria 's uneven development and fragmented public health architecture . <p> Take Edo State , for instance . With the Irrua Specialist Teaching Hospital serving as a leading treatment and research hub , Edo @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ necessarily because it is the most infected ; rather , it is the most visible . Its strength lies in detection , diagnosis , and reporting . In contrast , other states may be battling silent outbreaks , hidden by weak surveillance systems . The implication is clear : in Nigeria , data often reflects capacity -- not just reality . Strengthening institutions like the Nigeria Centre for Disease Control must therefore go beyond Abuja ; it must reach the grassroots . <p> Ondo State tells a different story , one rooted in ecology . Its agrarian communities , dense vegetation , and close human interaction with rodent populations make it a natural hotspot . Here , Lassa fever is not just a medical issue ; it is an environmental one . Food drying practices , poor housing , and inadequate waste management all create a perfect breeding ground for the disease . This underscores a critical point : Nigeria can not treat Lassa fever effectively without integrating environmental and agricultural policies into public health strategy . <p> In Ebonyi State , the challenge is access . Many patients arrive @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . The result is a higher fatality rate . This is not merely a failure of medicine ; it is a failure of systems . When primary healthcare is weak , diseases that are treatable become deadly . The availability of antivirals like Ribavirin means little if patients can not access care in time . <p> Bauchi State , on the other hand , highlights the danger of underreporting . With vast rural populations and limited surveillance infrastructure , many cases go undetected . This creates a false sense of security while the disease quietly spreads . It is a stark reminder that what is not reported is not necessarily absent . <p> Then there is Taraba State , where climate change and human mobility intersect . Shifting rainfall patterns , deforestation , and cross-border movements are altering disease dynamics . As communities expand into previously undisturbed habitats , they increase contact with disease vectors . Taraba is not just a hotspot ; it is a glimpse into Nigeria 's future if climate adaptation is not taken seriously . <p> What binds these five states together is not just geography @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ weak infrastructure , and environmental pressures converge . The concentration of Lassa fever cases in these areas is therefore not accidental -- it is diagnostic . Yet , Nigeria 's response has largely remained reactive . Each outbreak season triggers emergency meetings , press briefings , and short-term interventions . When the numbers decline , so does the urgency . This cycle of panic and neglect must end . <p> The lesson is simple but urgent : Nigeria must move from generalised response to precision public health . Resources should be strategically concentrated in high-risk zones , with permanent infrastructure rather than temporary measures . Regional treatment centres must be established beyond Edo , surveillance systems must be standardised nationwide , and community-level education must be sustained , not seasonal . More importantly , Lassa fever must be reframed as a development issue . It is about housing quality , waste management , food systems , education , and poverty . It is about how Nigerians live , not just how they fall ill . <p> There is also a political dimension that can not be ignored . Public health @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of disease , it raises questions about equity , resource allocation , and federal responsibility . Addressing Lassa fever effectively will require political will that transcends rhetoric . <p> Ultimately , the " five-state concentration " is not just a statistic -- it is a map of neglect and an opportunity for reform . If Nigeria can decisively fix the structural challenges in these hotspot states and use them as case studies for the remaining thirty-two subnational administrations , it can drastically reduce the national burden of Lassa fever . The choice before us is clear : continue reacting to outbreaks or confront the underlying conditions that make them inevitable . <p> All rights reserved . @@135925479 <p> In this report , DAYO OYEWO writes on how a privately owned forest in Osun State is drawing global attention for its role in preserving biodiversity and offering a model for conservation in Nigeria <p> Deep in the quiet rural landscape of Ikoyi in Osun State lies a stretch of forest that tells a rare story of resilience in a country where many natural ecosystems have steadily disappeared . <p> The Emerald Forest Reserve , a privately protected forest spanning about 300 acres , has now earned international recognition as a Key Biodiversity Area , a designation that places it among the world 's most important sites for biodiversity conservation . <p> The recognition marks a significant milestone not only for the forest itself but also for conservation efforts in Nigeria , particularly because the reserve is the first privately owned forest in the country to receive the designation . <p> The KBA certificate was formally presented at a ceremony held within the reserve in January , drawing conservation experts , researchers , community members and environmental advocates . <p> The Director-General of the Nigerian Conservation Foundation , Joseph @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ global Key Biodiversity Area Secretariat , said Key Biodiversity Areas are locations identified through rigorous scientific assessments because of their exceptional importance to the survival of species and ecosystems at a global level . <p> He added during the ceremony that the designation of the forest reserve as a KBA was triggered by the presence of the Ibadan malimbe . <p> The Ibadan malimbe is a rare bird species endemic to Nigeria , found only in the southwestern region of the country . Because it does not exist anywhere else in the world , the survival of the species is directly tied to the preservation of its habitat in this region . <p> " This species is endemic , meaning it exists only in Nigeria and nowhere else in the world . Even within Nigeria , it is restricted to the south-west . That makes it extremely vulnerable because whatever happens here directly affects the survival of the species globally . <p> " No matter how many of them we have today , they are already endangered . If anything goes wrong in this region , the species is lost @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ " Onoja added . <p> For conservationists , the discovery of the bird within the reserve immediately signalled the ecological importance of the forest . Extensive research followed , eventually leading to the site being formally recognised as a Key Biodiversity Area . <p> He noted that the designation also demonstrates that conservation initiatives in Nigeria can succeed through local efforts and collaboration . <p> " This gives hope that Nigerians can initiate conservation processes and complete them successfully . Other sites across the country , including freshwater ecosystems with endemic species , can also go through this process , " he said . <p> The forest 's co-director , Dr. Modupe Ladipo , received the certificate on behalf of the reserve and dedicated the recognition to the surrounding communities whose cooperation has helped protect the forest over the years . <p> " This achievement belongs to the community as much as it belongs to us . Without their support , it would have been difficult to preserve this forest , " she said . <p> Family vision across generations <p> The Emerald Forest Reserve did not begin as an @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ family commitment to preserving nature that spans nearly a century . <p> Director of the reserve , Professor Akin Abayomi , explained that the idea of protecting the forest evolved gradually through generations . <p> The family established a farm estate in Ibadan decades ago , and their connection to the land grew over time . When they later discovered the forested land in Ikoyi in the early 2000s , they were struck by how much natural vegetation still remained compared to the surrounding areas that had already been cleared . <p> At the time , the region was filled with sawmills as loggers harvested large quantities of timber . <p> Professor Abayomi said , " Our parents were farmers and agriculturalists . My grandfather worked in agriculture , and my father studied agriculture in the United Kingdom before returning to Nigeria to continue that work . When we first came here , most of the surrounding forests had already been cut down . We decided that this particular place should be preserved . " <p> Gradually , the family began acquiring parcels of land in the area , eventually @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ clearing the forest for farming or commercial development , they left it untouched to preserve a piece of what the natural ecosystem once looked like . <p> For Professor Abayomi , who is also the Lagos State Commissioner for Health , the disappearance of biodiversity is one of the greatest environmental threats facing humanity . <p> " When you step into this forest , you see what the environment used to look like . But once you step outside , you see how much has been lost . Every time a species goes extinct , it is almost like committing a crime against the universe . It is a crime against humanity and against future generations . Once a species disappears , our children will only see it in pictures or museums , " he further stated . <p> Scientific validation of biodiversity <p> The forest 's ecological value was confirmed through extensive research carried out by scientists and conservation organisations . <p> Professor Tayo Oyelowo of the Forestry Research Institute of Nigeria explained that a series of scientific assessments examined the forest 's plant composition , wildlife population , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ activity . <p> In some cases , the researchers discovered species that had already disappeared from other protected forests in Nigeria . <p> " We studied the plant species , animals , including insects , aquatic habitats and the soil ecosystem . Our findings revealed that the reserve contains a range of indigenous and endangered species . This is a private conservation area , yet its biodiversity is richer than that of some government-protected forests and national parks , " Oyelowo noted . <p> Providing more insight on the discovery of the Ibadan malimbe , Dr. Ekundayo Abayomi , who is also a co-director , said ornithologists from the University of Jos and the International Institute of Tropical Agriculture monitored the bird population in the forest for three years . <p> " Every few months , researchers visit the forest to record bird species , observe nesting patterns and track migratory birds that pass through the area during certain seasons . They also set up bird nets to capture and release birds in order to tag them for scientific monitoring . <p> " Through this process , researchers documented the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ birds were breeding within the forest . The discovery , according to her , generated excitement among conservationists . <p> " For years , scientists feared the species was disappearing because its habitat was being destroyed . So when researchers began to see them here , it was encouraging , " Ekundayo explained . " <p> Beyond birds , the forest is home to a wide variety of wildlife . Monkeys that had disappeared from surrounding areas have gradually returned to the reserve , forming several colonies . <p> There have also been sightings of pangolins , one of the most trafficked mammals in the world due to illegal wildlife trade . <p> During the KBA ceremony , conservationists released a rescued pangolin into the forest as part of ongoing wildlife protection efforts led by the Green Fingers Wildlife Conservation Initiative . <p> Challenges of preserving forest <p> Despite its ecological importance today , preserving the forest was not always easy . <p> On her part , Dr. Ladipo said illegal logging , poaching and staff retention were among the initial challenges encountered at the farm . <p> According to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ community leaders and law enforcement to stop the activities . <p> She said , " When we started , some people came in to cut trees and hunt animals . We had to appeal to the traditional ruler , and gradually , through diplomacy and sensitisation , the community began to understand the importance of protecting the forest . " <p> She added that retaining workers was also difficult due to the remote nature of the forest . <p> " It was challenging to keep staff because of the location . We experienced turnover and had to train workers repeatedly to understand what conservation entails . But by involving them and demonstrating the value of the forest , they became committed to the work , " she disclosed . <p> Future plans and sustainability <p> Speaking on future plans , Ladipo said efforts were underway to expand the forest and strengthen measures for its long-term protection . <p> She added that the initiative also aims to promote wider acceptance of conservation and encourage reforestation beyond the privately owned reserve , including on government-managed land . <p> According to her , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ step towards building lasting commitment to environmental sustainability . <p> " We are seeking support from local communities , government and international organisations to sustain the forest , expand conservation efforts and enhance ecosystem projects , " Modupe said . <p> " We do not want to be seen as beggars but as partners in preserving the planet . With the right collaborations , we can protect biodiversity , promote research and inspire other conservation initiatives across Nigeria and beyond , " she added . <p> The reserve introduced training programmes to create alternative sources of livelihood for residents in nearby villages , some of whom are also staff of the reserve . These include workshops in woodworking , beekeeping , textile production and craft-making . <p> On her part , Dr. Ekundayo said the aim was to demonstrate that protecting the forest could generate economic opportunities without destroying it . <p> Also , the forest has gradually transformed into a living laboratory for scientists and conservationists over the years . <p> " If you sell raw materials , you earn very little . But if you add value to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ fallen trees from natural storms are sometimes used to train community members in furniture making rather than being sold as raw timber , " Ekundayo explained . <p> " The reserve has also collaborated with universities and research institutions to support environmental studies . Students from the University of Ibadan regularly visit the forest to study biodiversity , soil composition and ecosystem dynamics . For many students , this is the closest natural forest they have ever seen , " she added . <p> Yet maintaining the reserve has required enormous personal sacrifice . <p> The directors , who are siblings , acknowledged that the project has largely been funded from their personal savings and pensions . <p> " All our savings have gone into this . But for us , wealth is not just money . Wealth is also the environment we leave behind for future generations , " Ekundayo disclosed . <p> Model for conservation <p> Looking ahead , the siblings hope the forest will become a model for conservation efforts across Nigeria . <p> Meanwhile , Professor Abayomi expressed concerns over the growing threat of deforestation , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ emphasised the role forests play in combating climate change . <p> " Deforestation is happening everywhere . If we can show that conservation works here , then it can be replicated in other places . Trees absorb carbon dioxide from the atmosphere and release oxygen , making forests essential in reducing global carbon emissions . This forest is like a farm that produces oxygen . We remove carbon dioxide from the air and store it in trees . " <p> For conservationists , protecting forests in Africa is increasingly seen as part of a global climate solution . <p> " Developed nations that produce high levels of carbon emissions may need to invest in preserving forests that act as carbon sinks . If the world industrialises everywhere , there will be nowhere left to absorb carbon . So protecting forests is not just about Nigeria -- it 's about the entire planet , " Abayomi explained . <p> The directors also hope the KBA recognition will provide stronger protection for the forest in the future . <p> " International recognition often helps prevent destructive development projects because it draws global @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ something like this is recognised globally , it becomes harder for anyone to destroy it , " Abayomi said . <p> However , they acknowledge that the long-term survival of the forest will depend on future generations continuing the work . <p> Regrowing a forest like Emerald , they say , would take up to two centuries if it were destroyed . <p> For now , the forest stands as a rare example of what can happen when individuals commit to protecting nature despite the odds . <p> In a country where forests are disappearing rapidly , the Emerald Forest Reserve offers a glimpse of a different future -- one in which conservation , community involvement and scientific research come together to safeguard biodiversity . <p> As visitors walk through its shaded trails , listening to the sounds of birds hidden in the canopy , the message becomes clear : sometimes , saving the planet begins with protecting a small patch of forest . <p> Dayo is a journalist at Punch Newspapers with over nine years of experience covering politics , entertainment , crime , and environmental conservation . @@135926112 <p> Energy security has always been a defining force in the evolution of civilisation and industrialisation . Nations with abundant and reliable access to energy resources consistently lead in technological advancement , economic growth , and societal development . The centrality of energy to daily life and business operations can not be overstated . For centuries , global energy supply has been dominated by carbon-based fossil resources -- coal , oil , and natural gas -- alongside hydroelectric power . Yet scientists and innovators have long recognised the potential of alternative energy sources : solar radiation captured through photovoltaic panels , the kinetic power of strong winds , geothermal heat from deep beneath the earth , and the conversion of organic waste into biogas through anaerobic digestion . In many communities , traditional biomass such as firewood still plays a significant role in meeting domestic energy needs . <p> On an industrial scale , the capacity to generate , transmit , and distribute energy is a key indicator of economic strength . Advanced and emerging economies -- including the United States , China , Germany , the United Kingdom , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Africa -- have built robust , reliable energy infrastructures . In these countries , energy is increasingly viewed as a multi-resource mix , integrated through smart grids that synchronise supply and demand in real time using digital technologies and data analytics . <p> Advertisement <p> With the turn of the millennium and the renewed global commitment to the Sustainable Development Goals , concerns over climate change , environmental degradation , and the finite nature of fossil fuels have intensified . This has accelerated the global shift toward renewable energy sources such as solar , wind , biomass , hydrogen , and geothermal . Integrating these diverse energy systems offers dual benefits : improving overall efficiency and supporting the transition to sustainable , low-carbon economies . <p> Global examples illustrate the growing momentum . For instance , the UK 's offshore wind-to-hydrogen projects like the Gigastack and the Humber cluster demonstrate how excess renewable energy can decarbonise heavy industry . Germany 's Energiewende integrates wind , solar , hydrogen , and district heating to stabilise a renewable-heavy grid . South Korea 's smart city projects use AI-driven energy management to coordinate @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ urban energy hubs combine electricity , heat , cooling , and storage to reduce emissions in megacities . The unique Denmark district heating networks utilise an integrated system of energy generated through a combined heat and power ( CHP ) plant from biomass , waste-to-energy , heat pumps , solar thermal , with wind power and thermal storage , enabling excess wind energy to be converted into heat . Similarly , Japan 's hydrogen-based smart communities demonstrate how electricity and gas systems can be co-optimised for resilience and carbon reduction . These examples show that integrated systems are not theoretical -- they are shaping real energy transitions . <p> Sustainability and Climate Imperatives <p> Climate action and the race for net zero emissions in most advanced and industrialised economies have increased the call for environmental responsibility in operational value chains of all sectors of the economy . Sustainability is the primary driver behind IES optimization . As global emissions continue to rise , integrated systems offer a pathway to deep decarbonisation by enabling higher renewable energy absorption through flexible demand and storage , reduced reliance on fossil fuels by electrifying @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ planning and operation . Other measures include improved resilience against climate-induced disruptions , enhanced environmental sustainability effort by focusing on heavy polluters through various industrial decarbonization measures . <p> The UNDP emphasises that the future of energy systems depends on flexibility and integration , not isolated renewable deployment , and must respond to evolving climate and economic conditions . Integrated systems also support circular-economy principles by reducing waste , optimising resource use , and enabling sector coupling . For example , using industrial waste heat for district heating as can be seen in Microsoft/Fortum 's data centre heat recycling in Helsinki , Finland , and Facebook 's data centre in Odense , Denmark . Similar waste heat reintegration is seen in industrial symbiosis in Kalundborg , Denmark , waste-to-energy plants like CopenHill , Copenhagen , and several other functional systems in the UK , Germany , and Austria . <p> Advertisement <p> Economic Optimisation and Market Design <p> Economic optimisation is central to IES development . Integrated systems reduce costs by sharing infrastructure across sectors ; reducing peak demand through coordinated load management , lowering operational costs through digital automation @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ streams such as grid services from electric vehicles or battery storage . <p> China 's carbon-reduction pathways highlight how carbon pricing , renewable portfolio standards , and flexible market operations support cost-effective IES deployment . In Europe , dynamic electricity pricing and cross-border energy markets allow renewable-rich regions to export surplus power , improving overall system economics . This is seen in a renewable-rich country like Denmark exporting excess wind energy to Norway and Germany through Nord Pool exchange . A similar scenario is seen in Portugal and Spain , shipping surplus solar power to France to stabilise the grid . <p> Other countries , regional hubs have developed similar cross-border energy transaction models , optimising the economic advantage of their renewable energy capabilities . Regional energy pools further demonstrate the economic value of integration . The Southern African Power Pool ( SAPP ) connects South Africa and Namibia with Zimbabwe and Zambia , and other countries in energy transactions . The West African Power Pool ( WAPP ) is a regional energy integration linking Nigeria , Ghana , Senegal , C ? te d'Ivoire , and others in ECOWAS @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ ( SIEPAC ) -- an integrated regional energy system -- with a 1,800 km transmission line connecting six countries from Guatemala to Panama , is another economic model enhancing renewable energy integration through a Regional Electricity Market ( MER ) for the offtake of surplus . Another example transitioning from a bilateral to a regional is the ASEAN Power Grid ( APG ) in Southeast Asia between Laos , Thailand , Malaysia , and Singapore . <p> Time-of-use tariffs also play a role . California 's aggregated home batteries and EVs provide grid stability , while the UK 's smart meter rollout encourages consumers to shift demand , reducing system costs and emissions . <p> Advertisement <p> Policy Frameworks and Governance <p> Policy is the backbone of integrated energy system optimisation . For energy integration to effectively harness the benefits of efficiency through absorption of renewable sources and drive meaningful decarbonisation , strong and clearly articulated government policy is essential . Effective governance requires long-term decarbonisation targets ( e.g. , EU Green Deal , UK Net Zero 2050 ) , regulatory support for sector coupling across electricity , heat , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and standards for digitalisation , cybersecurity , and data sharing . Market reforms must also reward system flexibility and low-carbon operation . <p> Advertisement <p> As energy systems become increasingly interconnected through ICT , IoT , and AI-enabled platforms , the cyber-attack surface expands . Policy frameworks must therefore embed " secure-by-design " principles to ensure resilience alongside integration . The World Economic Forum notes that the energy transition 's next challenge is not technology availability , but system-level integration supported by coherent policy frameworks . Without such policy alignment , even the most advanced technologies can not deliver their full potential . <p> The next decade will determine whether nations achieve their energy sufficiency and climate ambitions or fall behind . The energy transition is no longer about choosing technologies ; it is about connecting them and harnessing their combined effect . A cleaner , smarter , more resilient energy future is within reach . But it requires systems that talk to each other , technologies that work together , and policies that support integration . Integrated energy systems offer a proven pathway to resilience , affordability , and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . Governments must adopt whole-system strategies that unlock the full potential of renewable energy and digital innovation . The future of energy is flexible , and it starts with the choices we make today . <p> Wale Ajayi writes from Hull , East Yorkshire , England <p> Views expressed by contributors are strictly personal and not of TheCable.

@@135957431 <h> How Russia 's Multifaceted Relations Changing Egypt <h> How Russia 's Multifaceted Relations Changing Egypt <p> The Arab Republic of Egypt , a country spanning the northeast corner of Africa and the southwest corner of Asia , has a highly strategic location and attracts multifaceted interests of foreign players . For decades , Russia has established diplomatic relations with Egypt and has consistently sustained diverse ties with this country . It is no secret that Russia 's lust for the region is primarily due to the strategic importance of the Mediterranean Sea for investment and economic cooperation with the Maghreb region . <p> Determined to strengthen , particularly , economic cooperation , Russian President Vladimir Putin has maintained regular contacts with his colleague , President of Egypt , Abdel Fattah el-Sisi , mostly discussing both bilateral cooperation and broader regional developments . The current world 's geopolitical development , for instance , the United States-Israeli war on Iran in the Middle East , constitutes one theme both leaders frequently review , attempting to find long-term solutions . <p> On April 2 , Putin met with the Minister of Foreign @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Republic of Egypt , Badr Abdelatty , in the Kremlin -- the seat of Russia 's presidency . " <p> Vergos noted that nearly one billion tonnes of food , nearly one-fifth of all food available to consumers , went to waste in 2022 alone . What practical changes should businesses be making this year to stay relevant ? <p> Sustainability is entering an era where stakeholders are expecting corporate ' accountability ' , and they care more about the impact ( financial and non-financial ) of the wonderful sustainability disclosure documented in sustainability reports . This is driven partly by regulatory pressure but also the maturity and coordination in sustainability/ESG codification and standardization , especially with the alignment of non-financial performance ( E and S ) with financial performance and risks . Therefore , if a business is practicing ' sustainability ' , the leadership of the business ( the board and chief executives or even an individual entrepreneur , as is the case with a lot of SMEs in Nigeria ) have to understand the materiality issues of the business and integrate the management of those issues in the business strategy and goal setting @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ understand our sustainability issues and this is how we involve every part of the business to manage and contribute to the company 's goals ' . <p> Research shows that when we give women resources , we gain innovation . <p> Can you share a project where a woman-led initiative outperformed traditional methods because of a more inclusive approach ? <p> Yes , studies like UN Women ( 2024 ) : Facts and figures : Economic empowerment and International Monetary Fund ( 2024 ) : Chapter 9 : The Impact of Women 's Empowerment on Human Capital confirm the postulation which is evident even in everyday interactions that women have a ' multiplier effect ' . <p> From student projects I have supervised to development projects targeting economic growth and wealth creation I have seen first-hand that team that have a healthy mix of males and females and also people of different background ( academic and socio-economic ) often produce outstanding results . While I ca n't share the specific organisation but I will try to give you a description of the project . <p> An FMCG company decided to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ their value chain in a way that the company directly supported female run retail micro business by providing them with goods and a line of credit for the purchase of the company 's goods . @@136060440 <p> Mr Ghebreyesus explained that people today live longer and healthier lives on average than previous generations due to the power of science . <p> " Vaccines , penicillin , germ theory , MRI machines and the mapping of the human genome are just some of the achievements that science has delivered that have saved lives and transformed health for billions of people , " he said . <p> Despite these gains , the WHO warned that health threats are increasing due to climate change , environmental degradation , geopolitical tensions , and shifting demographics . <p> These challenges , it said , are contributing to persistent diseases , strained health systems , and emerging outbreaks with epidemic or pandemic potential . <h> Science behind health gains <p> The WHO emphasised that scientific progress , when widely adopted , has transformed healthcare delivery and outcomes . <p> It noted that before modern anaesthesia , surgery involved extreme pain , but advances in medicine , technology , and specialist training now allow safer procedures worldwide , including in resource-limited settings . <p> The organisation also highlighted that global immunisation efforts over @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ children , with vaccines contributing to a 40 per cent reduction in infant mortality . The measles vaccine alone has saved over 90 million lives . <p> In addition , improvements in early screening technologies , such as electronic blood pressure monitors and mammography for breast cancer , have become life-saving tools for millions of people . <h> Role in global health coordination <p> The WHO said it has played a central role in advancing science and global health coordination over its 78-year history . <p> It cited its response to the 2003 Severe Acute Respiratory Syndrome ( SARS ) outbreak , when it coordinated a global laboratory network that identified the virus within two weeks , setting a model for future outbreak responses . <p> The organisation also referenced its development of alcohol-based hand-rub formulations in 2009 , which have been widely adopted in healthcare settings to prevent infections , including during the COVID-19 pandemic . <p> It added that it continues to set global standards , including air quality guidelines to reduce risks of respiratory diseases and water safety standards to prevent illnesses such as cholera . <h> @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Health Day campaign , the WHO said it is convening key global events to strengthen collaboration . <p> These include a One Health Summit in Lyon , France , organised with the G7 Presidency , bringing together heads of state , scientists , and community leaders . <p> The organisation said it will also host a global forum of its collaborating centres , involving more than 800 academic and research institutions across over 80 countries . <p> The WHO Chief Scientist , Sylvie Briand noted that science transforms uncertainty into understanding and reveals the pathways to protect and heal our communities . <p> Ms Briand warned that without rigorous scientific inquiry , decision-making could be driven by bias and misinformation , leading to ineffective or harmful treatments . <h> One Health approach <p> In a message to mark the day , Nigeria 's First Lady Oluremi support science , invest in health systems , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ resilient world for all , " she said . <p> The WHO also called on governments , institutions , and individuals to ensure that evidence-based approaches guide health policies and everyday decisions , noting that sustained collaboration is essential to addressing current and future health challenges . <p> It maintained that progress in global health depends on countries working together to apply scientific knowledge in building stronger and more equitable health systems . 
@@136060883 <h> Youth Employment Initiatives in Nigeria : Grants , Training , Jobs and how to join <p> Nigeria is a country brimming with youth energy . Everywhere you turn there are young people with dreams , skills , and ideas . Yet , too many are stuck without proper opportunities , waiting for the right moment to rise . The government , NGOs , and private institutions have stepped in with programs aimed at bridging this gap . <p> From grants to skill acquisition , internships , and structured work experience , there is a wide range of pathways designed to empower young Nigerians . In this article , we will break it down street by street , program by program , with real dates , numbers , and steps on how to join . <p> This is the most comprehensive guide for youth employment initiatives in Nigeria in 2026 and beyond . <h> Grants and Financial Support : Money for Your Hustle <p> Let us talk money first because we all know that dreams without funding often stay dreams . There are a few major initiatives in Nigeria offering @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ not only provide capital but also mentorship and support to ensure your business idea stands a chance . <p> YEIDEP -- Youth Economic Intervention and De-Radicalization Programme . This initiative targets youths aged 18 to 35 . It has provided grants ranging from 50 thousand Naira to 500 thousand Naira to individuals with viable business ideas . The program also includes training workshops where you learn basic business management , entrepreneurship principles , and even marketing tactics . The Nigerian government rolled out expanded YEIDEP rounds in 2024 and continued in 2025 with thousands of beneficiaries across the country . Applications are typically online via the YEIDEP portal . You must be a Nigerian citizen with a valid NIN and your BVN ready . <p> Next up is the Nigeria Youth Investment Fund NYIF , a program aimed at youths between 18 and 40 . It provides a combination of training , mentorship , and access to loans or grants . NYIF 's structure is very straightforward . First , you apply online . Then you undergo basic business and financial literacy training . Once approved , you get funding @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ NYIF had invested hundreds of millions into youth-led ventures across Nigeria with a particular focus on tech , agriculture , and small-scale manufacturing . <p> There is also the YES Programme -- Youth Enterprise Support via Bank of Industry , which combines entrepreneurship education with direct access to funds . Many young Nigerians have used YES funding to start small businesses in Lagos , Abuja , Port Harcourt , and other hubs . The program is cyclical , with applications opening every year for selected sectors like food processing , agriculture , and digital services . <p> Even before these programs , Nigeria had YouWin ! , which historically offered grants to youth business plans . Although YouWin closed its earlier cycles by 2020 , its structure inspired a lot of the current programs . Its legacy remains in the form of mentorship structures and funding models that were later adopted by YEIDEP and NYIF . <h> Skills and Training : Learn to Earn <p> Funding is one part of the equation but without skills , money can be wasted . Fortunately , Nigeria has invested heavily in skill acquisition @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ 120 billion Naira youth skills acquisition program aimed at equipping tens of thousands of young people with practical , employable skills . These programs cover a range of sectors from agriculture , hospitality , fashion design , digital marketing , coding , and software development . They are often delivered through state training centers and partner institutions . The idea is to give youth practical skills plus starter kits or tools to immediately start their ventures or get employed . <p> Another initiative , the Youth Employability Booster Project YAI , trains thousands in vocational , digital , and entrepreneurial skills . The program focuses on bridging the gap between academic knowledge and real workplace demands . Many graduates have successfully used YAI training to find employment or start small businesses in Lagos , Kano , and Kaduna states . <p> The NITDA Digital States Initiative deserves special mention . This program equips youth with digital literacy and tech skills . It includes coding bootcamps , web development , app development , and basic cybersecurity training . Its goal is to prepare youth for the digital economy while boosting their @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ businesses . By 2025 , over 50,000 youths had passed through Digital States , with many landing remote work contracts for international clients . <p> Several NGOs and state programs , like the Youth Employment Pathways in the Niger Delta , combine skill acquisition with job placements and entrepreneurship guidance . This program is more localized and focuses on vulnerable youth in regions with high unemployment . Skills range from fisheries , agro-processing , welding , mechanics , and small-scale manufacturing . <h> Job Placement and Work Experience : Getting Paid While Learning <p> Not everyone wants to start a business immediately . Many youths prefer structured work experience with pay and exposure . Programs like N-Power , NSIP fill that gap . N-Power engages unemployed youth in agriculture , health , tech , teaching , and other sectors . Participants are paid monthly stipends while gaining practical work experience . N-Power has been operating since 2016 and has enrolled over 500,000 youth as of 2025 . The government regularly opens new application cycles with online registration . <p> Another notable program is the Nigeria Jubilee Fellows Programme 2.0 . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ placements in government , NGOs , and corporate institutions . Participants work on real projects , gaining skills and experience that are valuable for long-term career progression . Launched in late 2024 and continuing in 2025 , the program targets youth aged 25 to 35 . Applications are online , competitive , and evaluated based on merit and previous work experience . <p> Many state-level initiatives mimic these programs , providing young people with paid placements in hospitals , schools , local government offices , tech hubs , and community projects . The combined effect of these programs is to not only employ youth temporarily but also make them employable for future opportunities . <h> How to Join : The Playbook <p> Joining these programs is usually simple but requires attention to details . All major programs require Nigerian citizenship and valid identification numbers ( NIN and BVN ) . Age criteria differ but typically range from 18 to 35 or 18 to 40 depending on the program . <p> Online registration is the most common method . For N-Power , visit nasims.gov.ng , for NYIF , visit the fund @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ YEIDEP website . Always check the official deadlines . Applications usually open annually , sometimes twice a year depending on the program cycle . <p> Tips to increase your chances include preparing a clear statement of purpose , having a detailed business plan if required , ensuring all personal details match official documents , and completing all required training modules promptly if the program is multi-step . Networking with program alumni can also give insider tips and early notifications on openings . <h> Real Stories : Youths Who Made It <p> One documented example is Chinedu from Enugu . With mentorship and skill acquisition from NYIF , he scaled the business within a year to serve clients across Enugu state . <p> Another is Aisha from Kaduna who joined N-Power in 2023 . She got posted to a local health clinic where she gained practical experience in health administration and basic community health services . Some programs experience delays in disbursing funds . Others struggle with infrastructure or inconsistent training quality . There are complaints of favoritism or system glitches during online registration . <p> But the landscape is improving . Governments , NGOs , and private partners are investing more resources , improving monitoring , and updating online portals for easier access . Digital States will include AI , machine learning , and more advanced tech skill @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ programs , climate change projects , and community services .
